<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/10/21/how-the-sexual-effects-of-alcohol-marijuana-and-molly-compare"><time class="published" datetime="2019-10-21">October 21, 2019</time></a></span>

<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/10/21/how-the-sexual-effects-of-alcohol-marijuana-and-molly-compare">How the Sexual Effects of Alcohol, Marijuana, and Molly Compare</a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/10/21/how-the-sexual-effects-of-alcohol-marijuana-and-molly-compare"><time class="published" datetime="2019-10-21">October 21, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/10/21/how-the-sexual-effects-of-alcohol-marijuana-and-molly-compare" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="How the Sexual Effects of Alcohol, Marijuana, and Molly Compare" class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5da8d931a7874d5244db821d/1571668156184/smoking-up-drug-party.jpg" data-image-dimensions="847x565" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5da8d931a7874d5244db821d/1571668156184/smoking-up-drug-party.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5da8d931a7874d5244db821d/1571668156184/smoking-up-drug-party.jpg?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -28.2px; top: 0px; width: 169.4px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Alcohol, marijuana, and ecstasy/MDMA/Molly are among the most popular substances people use to enhance their sexual experiences. But when it comes to their effects, how similar or different are these drugs? </p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">A recent study published in the journal <em>Psychology and Sexuality</em> offers some insight.</p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/10/21/how-the-sexual-effects-of-alcohol-marijuana-and-molly-compare">Read More</a></span></div>

<div class="content-wrapper">
<div class="post">
<!--POST HEADER-->
<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/10/18/how-couples-can-sustain-a-strong-sexual-connection-for-a-lifetime"><time class="published" datetime="2019-10-18">October 18, 2019</time></a></span>
</span>
<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/10/18/how-couples-can-sustain-a-strong-sexual-connection-for-a-lifetime">How Couples Can Sustain a Strong Sexual Connection for a Lifetime</a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/10/18/how-couples-can-sustain-a-strong-sexual-connection-for-a-lifetime"><time class="published" datetime="2019-10-18">October 18, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/10/18/how-couples-can-sustain-a-strong-sexual-connection-for-a-lifetime" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="How Couples Can Sustain a Strong Sexual Connection for a Lifetime" class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5da09a3c94325279149d9e19/1571407250936/love-long-term-relationship-oziel-gomez-L8-0SAy-aoQ-unsplash.jpg" data-image-dimensions="2500x1667" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5da09a3c94325279149d9e19/1571407250936/love-long-term-relationship-oziel-gomez-L8-0SAy-aoQ-unsplash.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5da09a3c94325279149d9e19/1571407250936/love-long-term-relationship-oziel-gomez-L8-0SAy-aoQ-unsplash.jpg?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -28.2331px; top: -7.10543e-15px; width: 169.466px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">As a sex educator and researcher, one of the most common questions I get asked (besides “am I normal?”) is how a couple can stay sexually connected and passionate for the long term. It’s no surprise that this is such a common question when research shows that sexual passion typically declines over the course of a relationship. In fact, on average, sexual frequency and satisfaction start dropping <a href="https://www.lehmiller.com/blog/2015/12/17/how-does-sexual-satisfaction-change-over-time-in-relationships">after just one year</a>. </p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">However, this doesn’t mean that maintaining sexual passion or a satisfying sex life is impossible in a long-term relationship. So how do you do it? </p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/10/18/how-couples-can-sustain-a-strong-sexual-connection-for-a-lifetime">Read More</a></span></div>

<div class="post">
<!--POST HEADER-->
<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/10/16/what-you-think-you-know-about-the-male-refractory-period-is-probably-wrong"><time class="published" datetime="2019-10-16">October 16, 2019</time></a></span>
</span>
<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/10/16/what-you-think-you-know-about-the-male-refractory-period-is-probably-wrong">What You Think You Know About the Male Refractory Period is Probably Wrong</a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/10/16/what-you-think-you-know-about-the-male-refractory-period-is-probably-wrong"><time class="published" datetime="2019-10-16">October 16, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/10/16/what-you-think-you-know-about-the-male-refractory-period-is-probably-wrong" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="What You Think You Know About the Male Refractory Period is Probably Wrong" class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5da0dbc28fb8881a8ad31d64/1571235773090/male-symbol-erectile-dysfunction.jpg" data-image-dimensions="848x565" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5da0dbc28fb8881a8ad31d64/1571235773090/male-symbol-erectile-dysfunction.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5da0dbc28fb8881a8ad31d64/1571235773090/male-symbol-erectile-dysfunction.jpg?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -28.3px; top: 0px; width: 169.6px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">The refractory period is usually defined as a phase following orgasm during which additional orgasms are impossible. Sexual arousal is often said to be very difficult during this time as well. The refractory period is usually discussed in relation to men; women are often said to have no such period, and this difference has been used to explain why women are more likely than men to have multiple orgasms. </p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">This narrative—while extraordinarily popular and pervasive in human sexuality textbooks and websites—might be incorrect, though. The truth of the matter is that we don’t actually know all that much about the refractory period—it’s a “shortcoming” in the sexuality literature, so to speak.</p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/10/16/what-you-think-you-know-about-the-male-refractory-period-is-probably-wrong">Read More</a></span></div>

<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">

</span>
<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/10/14/nine-things-you-should-know-about-casual-sex">Nine Things You Should Know About Casual Sex</a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/10/14/nine-things-you-should-know-about-casual-sex"><time class="published" datetime="2019-10-14">October 14, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/10/14/nine-things-you-should-know-about-casual-sex" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="Nine Things You Should Know About Casual Sex" class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5da0b4f2d86e6f468ed47041/1571061630775/casual-sex-vidar-nordli-mathisen-f4OmS_SluJc-unsplash.jpg" data-image-dimensions="2500x1883" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5da0b4f2d86e6f468ed47041/1571061630775/casual-sex-vidar-nordli-mathisen-f4OmS_SluJc-unsplash.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5da0b4f2d86e6f468ed47041/1571061630775/casual-sex-vidar-nordli-mathisen-f4OmS_SluJc-unsplash.jpg?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -18.5133px; top: 0px; width: 150.027px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Casual sex is common, and studies suggest that most people will have it at one point or another. However, despite its prevalence, casual sex is widely misunderstood. Here are nine things science has taught us about casual sex that are worth knowing.</p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/10/14/nine-things-you-should-know-about-casual-sex">Read More</a></span></div>

<div class="content-wrapper">
<div class="post">
<!--POST HEADER-->
<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/10/11/why-its-important-to-recognize-differences-between-men-and-women"><time class="published" datetime="2019-10-11">October 11, 2019</time></a></span>
</span>
<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/10/11/why-its-important-to-recognize-differences-between-men-and-women">Why It's Important To Recognize Differences Between Men and Women</a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/10/11/why-its-important-to-recognize-differences-between-men-and-women"><time class="published" datetime="2019-10-11">October 11, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/10/11/why-its-important-to-recognize-differences-between-men-and-women" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="Why It's Important To Recognize Differences Between Men and Women" class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d9639da2085120a369d9260/1570802459650/gender-symbols-male-female.jpg" data-image-dimensions="692x692" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d9639da2085120a369d9260/1570802459650/gender-symbols-male-female.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d9639da2085120a369d9260/1570802459650/gender-symbols-male-female.jpg?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: 0px; top: 0px; width: 113px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">I’ve read a lot of media articles recently arguing that men and women really aren’t that different—and that any differences that do exist are due entirely to cultural conditioning. While I believe the authors of these articles are well intentioned and are striving for a noble goal of wanting to reduce gender inequality, I can’t help but feel that they are doing a disservice to readers by minimizing or (in some cases) completely ignoring important sex differences. The scientific reality is that men’s and women’s bodies are fundamentally different, and it’s in everyone’s interest to pay careful attention to those differences. </p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/10/11/why-its-important-to-recognize-differences-between-men-and-women">Read More</a></span></div>

<article class="hentry category-professional-issues-in-psychology tag-sex-positive tag-diversity tag-inclusivity author-justin-j-lehmiller post-type-text article-index-6" data-item-id="5d965ffef915ac636018c770" id="article-5d965ffef915ac636018c770">
<div class="content-wrapper">
<div class="post">
<!--POST HEADER-->
<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/10/9/what-does-it-mean-to-be-sex-positive"><time class="published" datetime="2019-10-09">October 09, 2019</time></a></span>
</span>
<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/10/9/what-does-it-mean-to-be-sex-positive">What Does It Mean To Be Sex Positive?</a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/10/9/what-does-it-mean-to-be-sex-positive"><time class="published" datetime="2019-10-09">October 09, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/10/9/what-does-it-mean-to-be-sex-positive" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="What Does It Mean To Be Sex Positive?" class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d965ffef915ac636018c770/1570629628014/copy-copy-tmwyw-promo-1.png" data-image-dimensions="1075x1013" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.2915121822033898" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d965ffef915ac636018c770/1570629628014/copy-copy-tmwyw-promo-1.png" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d965ffef915ac636018c770/1570629628014/copy-copy-tmwyw-promo-1.png?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -3.45805px; top: 0px; width: 119.916px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">What does it mean to be sex positive? This is one of those terms that different people define in different ways; however, at the core of most definitions is the idea that we must recognize that human sexuality—in its diverse and myriad forms of expression—is natural and healthy. Of course, there is an important caveat to this, which is that we’re only talking about consensual sexual expression here. Nonconsensual sex is another matter entirely. </p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Anyone who works in the areas of sex education, research, or therapy would do well to spend some time thinking about their own sex-positive philosophy because it’s vital that all of us approach our work in a non-judgmental fashion. </p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/10/9/what-does-it-mean-to-be-sex-positive">Read More</a></span></div>

<div class="content-wrapper">
<div class="post">
<!--POST HEADER-->
<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/10/7/older-women-who-date-younger-men-are-more-satisfied"><time class="published" datetime="2019-10-07">October 07, 2019</time></a></span>
</span>
<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/10/7/older-women-who-date-younger-men-are-more-satisfied">Older Women Who Date Younger Men Are More Satisfied </a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/10/7/older-women-who-date-younger-men-are-more-satisfied"><time class="published" datetime="2019-10-07">October 07, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/10/7/older-women-who-date-younger-men-are-more-satisfied" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="Older Women Who Date Younger Men Are More Satisfied " class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d964346f515f87c7bb09137/1570456826727/age-gap-relationship-older-woman-younger-man.jpg" data-image-dimensions="847x565" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d964346f515f87c7bb09137/1570456826727/age-gap-relationship-older-woman-younger-man.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d964346f515f87c7bb09137/1570456826727/age-gap-relationship-older-woman-younger-man.jpg?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -28.2px; top: 0px; width: 169.4px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Relationships in which a woman is significantly older than her male partner have always attracted a lot of attention and scrutiny. Case in point: remember what big news it was when Demi Moore and Ashton Kutcher were together? Or the international media obsession that followed French president Emmanuel Macron and his spouse, Brigitte, who happens to be 24 years his senior? </p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">This same scrutiny isn’t usually applied to relationships in which men are significantly older than their female partners. For example, U.S. President Donald Trump happens to be 24 years older than Melania. Certainly, the Trumps have attracted a lot of media attention—it just hasn’t been for their age difference! Research on age-gap relationships bears out this double standard.</p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/10/7/older-women-who-date-younger-men-are-more-satisfied">Read More</a></span></div>

<article class="hentry category-videos category-sex-research tag-porn tag-pornography tag-videos tag-teenagers author-justin-j-lehmiller post-type-text article-index-8" data-item-id="5d84e7af7d51593a02bf998f" id="article-5d84e7af7d51593a02bf998f">
<div class="content-wrapper">
<div class="post">
<!--POST HEADER-->
<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/27/how-porn-changes-the-way-teens-think-about-sex"><time class="published" datetime="2019-10-04">October 04, 2019</time></a></span>
</span>
<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/9/27/how-porn-changes-the-way-teens-think-about-sex">How Porn Changes The Way Teens Think About Sex, And Why We Need Porn Literacy</a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/27/how-porn-changes-the-way-teens-think-about-sex"><time class="published" datetime="2019-10-04">October 04, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/9/27/how-porn-changes-the-way-teens-think-about-sex" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="How Porn Changes The Way Teens Think About Sex, And Why We Need Porn Literacy" class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d84e7af7d51593a02bf998f/1570197620222/porn-computer-laptop.jpg" data-image-dimensions="846x566" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d84e7af7d51593a02bf998f/1570197620222/porn-computer-laptop.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d84e7af7d51593a02bf998f/1570197620222/porn-computer-laptop.jpg?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -27.9505px; top: 0px; width: 168.901px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">How does pornography affect the way that teens think about and approach sex? Is there a link between pornography and sexual violence, as many in the media have claimed? Also what’s the most effective way to talk to teens about sex and pornography in the first place? Dr. Emily Rothman thoughtfully addresses these questions and more in the TEDMed video below.   </p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Rothman’s talk is one of the best porn talks I’ve seen because it presents a very balanced take on the issue. It’s not an ideologically driven pro-porn or anti-porn take; rather, it’s an attempt to start a nuanced conversation about what we do and don’t know concerning the effects of porn and how we can use that knowledge to inform sex education efforts. </p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/9/27/how-porn-changes-the-way-teens-think-about-sex">Read More</a></span></div>

<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/10/2/in-nature-sexual-deception-is-everywhere-you-look"><time class="published" datetime="2019-10-02">October 02, 2019</time></a></span>
</span>
<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/10/2/in-nature-sexual-deception-is-everywhere-you-look">In Nature, Sexual Deception is Everywhere You Look</a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/10/2/in-nature-sexual-deception-is-everywhere-you-look"><time class="published" datetime="2019-10-02">October 02, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/10/2/in-nature-sexual-deception-is-everywhere-you-look" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="In Nature, Sexual Deception is Everywhere You Look" class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d8be31febe4bc2fc5f6c466/1570024828099/pinnochios-lined-up.jpg" data-image-dimensions="848x565" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d8be31febe4bc2fc5f6c466/1570024828099/pinnochios-lined-up.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d8be31febe4bc2fc5f6c466/1570024828099/pinnochios-lined-up.jpg?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -28.3px; top: 0px; width: 169.6px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">A few weeks ago, <a href="https://www.lehmiller.com/blog/2019/8/16/the-flower-that-tricks-insects-into-having-sex-with-it"><span style="text-decoration:underline">I posted an article</span></a> about the beautiful—but sexually deceitful—orchid. Some orchids have evolved to mimic the appearance and scent of female bees in order to lure male bees, which then attempt to have sex with the flowers. This is in the orchid’s interest, of course, because the bees’ actions actually help the orchid to reproduce through pollination. However, it’s not necessarily in the interest of the male bees. </p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">This kind of sexual deception is hardly unique to orchids—in fact, you can find evidence of it throughout nature.</p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/10/2/in-nature-sexual-deception-is-everywhere-you-look">Read More</a></span></div>

<div class="content-wrapper">
<div class="post">
<!--POST HEADER-->
<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/30/the-erotic-equation-and-why-were-drawn-to-people-who-play-hard-to-get"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-30">September 30, 2019</time></a></span>
</span>
<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/9/30/the-erotic-equation-and-why-were-drawn-to-people-who-play-hard-to-get">The Erotic Equation and Why We’re Drawn to People Who Play Hard to Get</a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/30/the-erotic-equation-and-why-were-drawn-to-people-who-play-hard-to-get"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-30">September 30, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/9/30/the-erotic-equation-and-why-were-drawn-to-people-who-play-hard-to-get" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="The Erotic Equation and Why We’re Drawn to People Who Play Hard to Get" class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d8ccfdabb271b09d0104e0c/1569852058134/playing-hard-to-get-quote.jpg" data-image-dimensions="848x565" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d8ccfdabb271b09d0104e0c/1569852058134/playing-hard-to-get-quote.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d8ccfdabb271b09d0104e0c/1569852058134/playing-hard-to-get-quote.jpg?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -28.3px; top: 0px; width: 169.6px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">In Jack Morin’s classic book <em>The Erotic Mind</em>, he describes the “erotic equation” as follows:  </p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;"><strong>Attraction + Obstacles = Excitement</strong></p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">What this equation means is basically that if we’re attracted to something and we’re told we can’t have it, this makes us come to want it even more. It’s kind of like the phenomenon of reverse psychology, and it seems to be a basic part of human nature. Indeed, there’s a lot of evidence that this is true when it comes to both our sexual turn-ons and the romantic partners we’re drawn to. </p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/9/30/the-erotic-equation-and-why-were-drawn-to-people-who-play-hard-to-get">Read More</a></span></div>

<div class="content-wrapper">
<div class="post">
<!--POST HEADER-->
<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/27/porn-ed-what-happens-when-porn-replaces-sex-education"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-27">September 27, 2019</time></a></span>
</span>
<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/9/27/porn-ed-what-happens-when-porn-replaces-sex-education">Porn Ed: What Happens When Porn Replaces Sex Education?</a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/27/porn-ed-what-happens-when-porn-replaces-sex-education"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-27">September 27, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/9/27/porn-ed-what-happens-when-porn-replaces-sex-education" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="Porn Ed: What Happens When Porn Replaces Sex Education?" class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d8d00c249214a7d28cd8917/1569592823741/Screen+Shot+2019-09-26+at+2.23.06+PM.png" data-image-dimensions="1962x1298" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d8d00c249214a7d28cd8917/1569592823741/Screen+Shot+2019-09-26+at+2.23.06+PM.png" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d8d00c249214a7d28cd8917/1569592823741/Screen+Shot+2019-09-26+at+2.23.06+PM.png?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -28.9029px; top: 0px; width: 170.806px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Adolescents today have greater access to a wider range of pornography than any previous generation. So how is it affecting them?  </p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">This question is the crux of a new episode of Lisa Ling’s CNN series <a href="https://www.cnn.com/videos/health/2019/09/25/this-is-life-lisa-ling-porn-ed-1.cnn"><span style="text-decoration:underline"><em>This is Life</em></span></a><em> </em>titled “Porn Ed,” which premieres September 29. Ling wanted to better understand how accessibility to porn is changing the way adolescents and young adults think about sex and approach relationships.  </p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">I had a chance to preview the episode and sit down with Ling to discuss it. </p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/9/27/porn-ed-what-happens-when-porn-replaces-sex-education">Read More</a></span></div>

<article class="hentry category-sexologist-interviews tag-adhd tag-interviews tag-gender-differences tag-sexual-orientation tag-sexual-frequency author-justin-j-lehmiller post-type-text article-index-12" data-item-id="5d84f541e707a67e06e6a8b2" id="article-5d84f541e707a67e06e6a8b2">
<div class="content-wrapper">
<div class="post">
<!--POST HEADER-->
<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/25/do-people-with-adhd-have-a-harder-time-with-monogamy"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-25">September 25, 2019</time></a></span>
</span>
<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/9/25/do-people-with-adhd-have-a-harder-time-with-monogamy">Do People With ADHD Have a Harder Time With Monogamy? </a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/25/do-people-with-adhd-have-a-harder-time-with-monogamy"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-25">September 25, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/9/25/do-people-with-adhd-have-a-harder-time-with-monogamy" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="Do People With ADHD Have a Harder Time With Monogamy? " class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d84f541e707a67e06e6a8b2/1569420018139/cheating-behind-her-back.jpg" data-image-dimensions="450x300" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d84f541e707a67e06e6a8b2/1569420018139/cheating-behind-her-back.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d84f541e707a67e06e6a8b2/1569420018139/cheating-behind-her-back.jpg?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -28.25px; top: 0px; width: 169.5px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Do people with attention-deficit hyperactivity disorder (ADHD) have a more difficult time being monogamous? Are they more inclined to be in sexually open relationships? </p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Sex therapist Ari Tuckman just published a book titled <a href="https://amzn.to/31ldpXm" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline"><em>ADHD After Dark</em></span></a> that explores these questions, as well as how ADHD impacts people’s sex lives and relationships more broadly. For this book, Tuckman surveyed more than 3,000 people who were in relationships in which one partner has ADHD and the other doesn’t in order to better understand when these relationships work—and when they don’t.  </p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">One of the things Tuckman’s survey revealed was that persons with ADHD were more likely to have participated in both consensual nonmonogamy (i.e., being in some kind of sexually open relationship) <em>and</em> nonconsensual nonmonogamy (i.e., cheating or infidelity). Persons with ADHD also expressed more interest in having an open relationship compared to their non-ADHD counterparts. </p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/9/25/do-people-with-adhd-have-a-harder-time-with-monogamy">Read More</a></span></div>

<div class="content-wrapper">
<div class="post">
<!--POST HEADER-->
<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/23/announcing-the-7th-annual-spsp-sexuality-pre-conference"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-23">September 23, 2019</time></a></span>
</span>
<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/9/23/announcing-the-7th-annual-spsp-sexuality-pre-conference">Announcing the 7th Annual SPSP Sexuality Pre-Conference!</a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/23/announcing-the-7th-annual-spsp-sexuality-pre-conference"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-23">September 23, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/9/23/announcing-the-7th-annual-spsp-sexuality-pre-conference" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="Announcing the 7th Annual SPSP Sexuality Pre-Conference!" class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d84dbf14745226d846b1e9f/1569418605538/Screen+Shot+2019-09-20+at+10.03.31+AM.png" data-image-dimensions="758x233" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="500w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d84dbf14745226d846b1e9f/1569418605538/Screen+Shot+2019-09-20+at+10.03.31+AM.png" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d84dbf14745226d846b1e9f/1569418605538/Screen+Shot+2019-09-20+at+10.03.31+AM.png?format=500w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -127.307px; top: 0px; width: 367.614px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">I am pleased to announce the seventh annual SPSP Sexuality Pre-Conference! It will be held next February prior to the annual meeting of the Society for Personality and Social Psychology. Our last six pre-conferences were wildly successful and we are excited to further build on this tradition.</p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">The next <a href="http://meeting.spsp.org/preconferences/sexuality" target="_blank">SPSP Sexuality Pre-Conference</a> will take place on Thursday, February 27, 2020 at the Hyatt Regency in New Orleans. The theme will be <em>"sex on the margins." </em> We selected this theme in order to highlight sexual practices, movements, and identities that are often overlooked in mainstream research. Among the topics that will be highlighted in this pre-conference are bisexuality and Black women’s sexuality. </p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/9/23/announcing-the-7th-annual-spsp-sexuality-pre-conference">Read More</a></span></div>

<div class="post">
<!--POST HEADER-->
<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/13/the-science-of-sperm-how-this-microscopic-organism-travels-great-distances"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-20">September 20, 2019</time></a></span>

<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/9/13/the-science-of-sperm-how-this-microscopic-organism-travels-great-distances">The Science of Sperm: How This Microscopic Organism Travels Great Distances</a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/13/the-science-of-sperm-how-this-microscopic-organism-travels-great-distances"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-20">September 20, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/9/13/the-science-of-sperm-how-this-microscopic-organism-travels-great-distances" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="The Science of Sperm: How This Microscopic Organism Travels Great Distances" class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d6e8c3c847c8300017538f0/1568988047786/sperm-cgi.jpg" data-image-dimensions="872x549" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d6e8c3c847c8300017538f0/1568988047786/sperm-cgi.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d6e8c3c847c8300017538f0/1568988047786/sperm-cgi.jpg?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -33.2413px; top: 0px; width: 179.483px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Sperm and other microscopic organisms don’t have an easy time traveling from Point A to Point B. When an organism is that small, traveling just one inch is actually an incredible undertaking because they’re fighting against much more resistance. So how do they do it? </p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/9/13/the-science-of-sperm-how-this-microscopic-organism-travels-great-distances">Read More</a></span></div>

<article class="hentry category-contraception-safer-sex tag-hpv tag-vaccine tag-stds tag-health author-justin-j-lehmiller post-type-text article-index-15" data-item-id="5d7aa263fc5ff71eabdbda11" id="article-5d7aa263fc5ff71eabdbda11">
<div class="content-wrapper">
<div class="post">
<!--POST HEADER-->
<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/18/the-hpv-vaccine-is-underutilized-and-how-we-can-change-that"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-18">September 18, 2019</time></a></span>
</span>
<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/9/18/the-hpv-vaccine-is-underutilized-and-how-we-can-change-that">The HPV Vaccine is Underutilized--And How We Can Change That</a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/18/the-hpv-vaccine-is-underutilized-and-how-we-can-change-that"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-18">September 18, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/9/18/the-hpv-vaccine-is-underutilized-and-how-we-can-change-that" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="The HPV Vaccine is Underutilized--And How We Can Change That" class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d7aa263fc5ff71eabdbda11/1568815231414/vaccine-shot-injection.jpg" data-image-dimensions="848x565" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d7aa263fc5ff71eabdbda11/1568815231414/vaccine-shot-injection.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d7aa263fc5ff71eabdbda11/1568815231414/vaccine-shot-injection.jpg?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -28.3px; top: 0px; width: 169.6px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">The human papillomavirus (HPV) is the most common sexually transmitted infection and it’s responsible for a number of negative health effects. In addition to genital warts, it has the potential to cause a number of cancers, including those of the cervix, anus, and throat. A vaccine that can prevent HPV (and, therefore, its associated health problems) has been around for nearly a decade; however, it continues to be widely underutilized in the United States.</p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/9/18/the-hpv-vaccine-is-underutilized-and-how-we-can-change-that">Read More</a></span></div>

</span>

<article class="hentry category-professional-issues-in-psychology tag-open-access tag-publishing tag-copyright author-justin-j-lehmiller post-type-text article-index-16" data-item-id="5d7a9ee2c4794d2eaa104d06" id="article-5d7a9ee2c4794d2eaa104d06">
<div class="content-wrapper">
<div class="post">
<!--POST HEADER-->
<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/16/why-i-deleted-my-academia-edu-account-and-why-you-should-too"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-16">September 16, 2019</time></a></span>
</span>
<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/9/16/why-i-deleted-my-academia-edu-account-and-why-you-should-too">Why I Deleted My Academia.edu Account and Why You Should, Too</a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/16/why-i-deleted-my-academia-edu-account-and-why-you-should-too"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-16">September 16, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/9/16/why-i-deleted-my-academia-edu-account-and-why-you-should-too" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="Why I Deleted My Academia.edu Account and Why You Should, Too" class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d7a9ee2c4794d2eaa104d06/1568643483879/delete-button-keyboard.jpg" data-image-dimensions="848x565" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d7a9ee2c4794d2eaa104d06/1568643483879/delete-button-keyboard.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d7a9ee2c4794d2eaa104d06/1568643483879/delete-button-keyboard.jpg?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -28.3px; top: 0px; width: 169.6px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">The traditional model of academic publishing restricts access to research by putting it in the hands of private companies. As a result, I’ve had to work hard to make my research available to those who wish to read it. I’ve done so by publishing accessible summaries on this blog, by publishing as much as I can in open-access journals, and by establishing profiles on file-sharing sites like Academia.Edu and ResearchGate, which allow you to store and share full-text uploads of papers with anyone. I will keep doing the first two going forward, but I’ve grown leery of the latter and have increasingly come to realize that these file-sharing websites aren’t an effective solution to the problems of academic publishing. In fact, I’ve grown quite concerned about these sites and have come to realize that academics need to pursue other means of sharing their work. Let me explain. </p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/9/16/why-i-deleted-my-academia-edu-account-and-why-you-should-too">Read More</a></span></div>
<!--POST FOOTER-->

<div class="content-wrapper">
<div class="post">
<!--POST HEADER-->
<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/11/how-adhd-affects-peoples-sex-and-love-lives"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-11">September 11, 2019</time></a></span>
</span>
<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/9/11/how-adhd-affects-peoples-sex-and-love-lives">ADHD After Dark: How ADHD Affects People’s Sex and Love Lives</a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/11/how-adhd-affects-peoples-sex-and-love-lives"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-11">September 11, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/9/11/how-adhd-affects-peoples-sex-and-love-lives" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="ADHD After Dark: How ADHD Affects People’s Sex and Love Lives" class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d6f03ba76e3ba0001859435/1568994625202/adhd-word-dictionary.jpg" data-image-dimensions="848x565" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d6f03ba76e3ba0001859435/1568994625202/adhd-word-dictionary.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d6f03ba76e3ba0001859435/1568994625202/adhd-word-dictionary.jpg?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -28.3px; top: 0px; width: 169.6px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">How does attention-deficit hyperactivity disorder (ADHD) affect people in adult life? Specifically, what are the implications for people’s sexual and romantic relationships? </p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">For the answers to these questions, I spoke with a certified sex therapist, Dr. Ari Tuckman. He has just published a book titled <a href="https://amzn.to/31ldpXm" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline"><em>ADHD After Dark</em></span></a>, which explores how adult ADHD affects people in the bedroom and beyond. For this book, Tuckman surveyed more than 3,000 people who were in relationships in which one partner has ADHD and the other doesn’t in order to better understand when these relationships work—and when they don’t.  </p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/9/11/how-adhd-affects-peoples-sex-and-love-lives">Read More</a></span></div>

<div class="post">
<!--POST HEADER-->
<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/9/science-fiction-sex-fantasies"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-09">September 09, 2019</time></a></span>
</span>
<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/9/9/science-fiction-sex-fantasies">Science Fiction Sex Fantasies: Why Some People are Turned on by Impossible Scenarios</a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/9/science-fiction-sex-fantasies"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-09">September 09, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/9/9/science-fiction-sex-fantasies" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="Science Fiction Sex Fantasies: Why Some People are Turned on by Impossible Scenarios" class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d6eb9cc7398800001b4134b/1568037637419/robot-kiss.jpg" data-image-dimensions="848x565" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d6eb9cc7398800001b4134b/1568037637419/robot-kiss.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d6eb9cc7398800001b4134b/1568037637419/robot-kiss.jpg?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -28.3px; top: 0px; width: 169.6px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">A large number of people have sexual fantasies that are impossible to act out because they feature elements of science fiction. As some evidence of this, I surveyed more than 4,000 Americans about their sexual fantasies for my book <a href="https://amzn.to/2ZKwhOr" target="_blank">Tell Me What You Want</a> and found that more than half of my respondents had fantasized about a fictional character from a book or movie, one-third had fantasized about a mythical creature (like a vampire or werewolf), one-quarter had fantasized about a cartoon or anime character, and one-sixth had fantasized about sex with an alien. That’s a lot of sci-fi sex!</p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/9/9/science-fiction-sex-fantasies">Read More</a></span></div>

<article class="hentry category-videos tag-disability tag-videos tag-intellectual-disabilities author-justin-j-lehmiller post-type-text article-index-20" data-item-id="5d670a708fc07e00015eff32" id="article-5d670a708fc07e00015eff32">
<div class="content-wrapper">
<div class="post">
<!--POST HEADER-->
<header>
<span class="article-dateline-above-title">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/6/sex-and-disability-intellectual-disabilities-and-the-right-to-sexuality"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-06">September 06, 2019</time></a></span>
</span>
<h1 class="entry-title" data-content-field="title"><a href="/blog/2019/9/6/sex-and-disability-intellectual-disabilities-and-the-right-to-sexuality">Sex and Disability: Intellectual Disabilities and the Right to Sexuality</a></h1>
<span class="article-dateline">
<span class="date"><a href="/blog/2019/9/6/sex-and-disability-intellectual-disabilities-and-the-right-to-sexuality"><time class="published" datetime="2019-09-06">September 06, 2019</time></a><span class="delimiter">/</span></span>
</span>
<span class="article-byline">
<span class="location"></span>
<span class="author"><a href="/?author=51e46531e4b0b50a199639bb" rel="author">Justin J Lehmiller</a></span>
</span>
</header>
<!--POST BODY-->
<div class="excerpt-thumb">
<div class="intrinsic">
<div class="content">
<a class="excerpt-image content-fill" href="/blog/2019/9/6/sex-and-disability-intellectual-disabilities-and-the-right-to-sexuality" style="overflow: hidden;"><img alt="Sex and Disability: Intellectual Disabilities and the Right to Sexuality" class="" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d670a708fc07e00015eff32/1567778431736/sex-and-disability.png" data-image-dimensions="1073x1010" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d670a708fc07e00015eff32/1567778431736/sex-and-disability.png" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/51e46531e4b0b50a199639bd/51e4680fe4b07d809db56c2c/5d670a708fc07e00015eff32/1567778431736/sex-and-disability.png?format=300w" style="font-size: 0px; left: -3.52426px; top: 0px; width: 120.049px; height: 113px; position: relative;"/></a>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="body entry-content excerpt-content"><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">In many countries, including the United States, persons with intellectual disabilities are deemed unable to provide sexual consent. The argument behind this is usually that these individuals cannot understand the potential risks of sex and, therefore, they cannot offer informed consent. So does this mean that everyone with an intellectual deficit must necessarily remain celibate lifelong? </p><span class="inline-action"><a class="inline-read-more" href="/blog/2019/9/6/sex-and-disability-intellectual-disabilities-and-the-right-to-sexuality">Read More</a></span></div>
